# Proposed OEIS comments: A235217 to A237844

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A235217

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235217>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [7237](../papers/7001-7500/7237-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 16*a(n-1) +52*a(n-2) -188*a(n-3) -10*a(n-4) +188*a(n-5) -29*a(n-6) -40*a(n-7) +10*a(n-8)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 28 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235239

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235239>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [412](../papers/0001-0500/0412-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k (the k=4..6 recurrence works also for k=1..3; apparently all rows and columns satisfy the same order 41 recurrence):

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235280

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235280>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2990](../papers/2501-3000/2990-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: T(n,k) is the number of (n+1) X (k+1) 0..3+i arrays with every 2 X 2 subblock having its diagonal sum differing from its antidiagonal sum by 3+2*i, for i=1..3(..?).
> Empirical for column k (the k=2..9 recurrence also works for k=1; apparently all rows and columns satisfy the same order 9 recurrence):

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [7504](../papers/7501-8000/7504-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) +3*a(n-2) -15*a(n-3) +4*a(n-4) +18*a(n-5) -12*a(n-6).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235289

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235289>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3186](../papers/3001-3500/3186-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: T(n,k) is the number of (n+1) X (k+1) 0..2+i arrays with every 2 X 2 subblock having its diagonal sum differing from its antidiagonal sum by 2+2*i (constant-stress 1 X 1 tilings) for i=1..4(..?).
> Empirical for column k (the recurrence for k=2..7 also works for k=1; apparently every row and column satisfies the same order 6 recurrence):

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235896

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235896>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [7901](../papers/7501-8000/7901-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 13*a(n-1) - 44*a(n-2) - 24*a(n-3) + 192*a(n-4) + 144*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(125 - 984*x + 432*x^2 + 5760*x^3 + 3888*x^4) / ((1 - 6*x)*(1 - 4*x - 4*x^2)*(1 - 3*x - 6*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 27 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:


> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-2) + 2*a(n-3) + 8*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: 4*x*(4 + 8*x + 11*x^2 + 14*x^3) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 + 2*x + 3*x^2 + 4*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A236407

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236407>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9022](../papers/9001-9500/9022-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(n-3)*a(n) -4*(2*n-1)*(n-3)*a(n-1) +2*(7*n^2-28*n+20)*a(n-2) -4*(n-1)*(2*n-7)*a(n-3) +(n-1)*(n-4)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 01 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
